### Dive corrections

- 2026-09-25 Stage-2b deep dive. Separated five heterogeneous runs from the two main graded papers, retained logistical human interventions, pinned live artifacts and commits, qualified evaluator independence, and found no peer-reviewed acceptance record for the study itself. 2026-09-25 Stage-4 closeout: autonomous-science evidence must separate execution, artifact integrity, manuscript quality, venue prediction, and scientific validity; none substitutes for another.

### Verdict

This is strong artifact-backed early evidence that frontier agents can execute the engineering of open-ended AI research yet fail expert judgments on two selected questions. The credible denominator is two main acceptance-style evaluations, not five comparable trials, and no AI Scientist-specific reliability rate follows.

### Dive corrections

- 2026-09-25 Stage 2c: paired closed issue #31641 with merged fix #31667; REATTRIBUTED the initial FP4-page hypothesis to strided Q and NARROWED validation to focused reruns plus merge state.

### Verdict

Treat concurrency as a correctness dimension and enforce layout contracts before any custom CPU/HIP kernel call; do not port the XQA code itself.

- updated `dflash2-block-drafter-experimental-build.md`

   - *unaffected*: Merged diff and config enforce a distinct DFlash2 V2 path with convolution and selector.

- 2026-09-25 Stage 2d: CONFIRMED the matched DFlash2-versus-DSpark performance receipts and replay artifacts; NARROWED K8 from a global winner to the c1 objective winner; NARROWED quality parity to three matched families with a small agentic difference; separated the core-subset throughput row from the matched 17-run protocol; confirmed no EXL3/CPU/gfx90a evidence. 1. On committed real-prompt summaries, EXL3 and NVFP4 are similar on prose c1 (19.1 vs 18.8 tok/s), NVFP4 is faster on code c1 (52.2 vs 48.6), and EXL3 reports higher aggregate mixed c4 throughput (43.4 vs 31.4) with lower TTFT (0.66 vs 1.97 s).
   - *unaffected*: Committed summaries and category artifacts support the real-prompt rates.
   - anchor `results/summary.md:4-6` @ tonyd2wild/GLM-5.3-Flash-EXL3-on-2x-NVIDIA-DGX-Spark@dc91a125fc60349ce99498d65dac5bc772a43c54 (retrieved 2026-09-25): "| prose decode, c1, tok/s (median of 3 runs; range) | 18.8 (18.8–19.8) | 19.1 (18.3–19.5) |"
   - anchor `results/summary.md:5` @ tonyd2wild/GLM-5.3-Flash-EXL3-on-2x-NVIDIA-DGX-Spark@dc91a125fc60349ce99498d65dac5bc772a43c54 (retrieved 2026-09-25): "| code decode, c1, tok/s (median of 3 runs; range) | 52.2 (48.3–57.7) | 48.6 (41.9–50.2) |"
   - anchor `results/summary.md:6` @ tonyd2wild/GLM-5.3-Flash-EXL3-on-2x-NVIDIA-DGX-Spark@dc91a125fc60349ce99498d65dac5bc772a43c54 (retrieved 2026-09-25): "| mixed real-prompt load c4: aggregate tok/s / TTFT | 31.4 / 1.97 s | 43.4 / 0.66 s |"
2. Cold and cached work separate sharply: at a fresh 211,001-token prompt NVFP4 reports 2,763 tok/s versus EXL3 1,752, while replay is 9.2 s versus 0.8 s. The replay row is prefix-cache behavior, not prefill throughput.
   - *reattributed*: The 0.8/9.2-second row is prefix replay, not cold prefill.
   - anchor `results/summary.md:22` @ tonyd2wild/GLM-5.3-Flash-EXL3-on-2x-NVIDIA-DGX-Spark@dc91a125fc60349ce99498d65dac5bc772a43c54 (retrieved 2026-09-25): "| cold prefill on a fresh 211,001-token prompt, tok/s | 2,763 | 1,752 | |"
   - anchor `results/summary.md:23` @ tonyd2wild/GLM-5.3-Flash-EXL3-on-2x-NVIDIA-DGX-Spark@dc91a125fc60349ce99498d65dac5bc772a43c54 (retrieved 2026-09-25): "| 211,001-token context replayed (prefix cache) | 9.2 s | 0.8 s | |"
   - anchor `results/summary.md:10` @ tonyd2wild/GLM-5.3-Flash-EXL3-on-2x-NVIDIA-DGX-Spark@dc91a125fc60349ce99498d65dac5bc772a43c54 (retrieved 2026-09-25): "Prefill is quoted cold only; cache replay has its own labeled row."
3. The 40-prompt, three-run automatic-score battery does not show a consistent EXL3 quality advantage: scores are NVFP4 0.855/0.876/0.810 and EXL3 0.846/0.789/0.815. A separate blind pairwise judge over six five-prompt categories (30 prompts; reasoning and JSON excluded) totals 9 NVFP4 wins, 8 EXL3 wins and 13 ties.

### Dive corrections

- 2026-09-25 Stage 2e: CONFIRMED the extensive committed battery; REATTRIBUTED cached replay away from prefill; NARROWED quality to this 40-prompt battery; NARROWED performance attribution because node pairs and configs differ; confirmed no CPU/ROCm evidence. Second reader corrected the judge denominator: 40 prompts for automatic scoring, 30 prompts across six categories for pairwise judging.

### Verdict

Use this as a strong workload and receipt design plus an operational GB10 comparison. Do not use it as a controlled proof that EXL3 is faster or higher quality than NVFP4.

### Dive corrections

- 2026-09-25 Stage 2e: CONFIRMED W4A16 as a distinct EXL3 alternative and verified final raw throughput/backend/acceptance receipts; NARROWED the 56–69% comparison due mismatched configs and n=1 rival cells; NARROWED quality to external card values plus a two-item local smoke; confirmed no AMD execution. Second reader recomputed the four medians from all 12 committed per-wave files and added the missing same-hardware/config-confound anchors; no key claim changed.

### Verdict

Treat W4A16 as a real alternative to EXL3 and adopt its raw throughput receipt shape. Do not treat the three-way headline as proof that W4A16 is intrinsically faster because rival denominators and configs differ.

- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): Project-derived candidate: require a matched local quality battery before selecting either codec.
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): Project-derived candidate: reproduce the raw-receipt layout for CPU/MI210 c1/c2/c4/c6 runs.

- 2026-09-25 Stage 2e: CONFIRMED artifact identity, mixed tensor inventory, stored size and dtype census; NARROWED runtime-VRAM and throughput claims to author-reported five-prompt measurements without raw receipts; NARROWED reproducibility because the named calibration/export scripts are not published in the model artifact; confirmed NVIDIA Blackwell-only execution scope. ### Verdict

Use the mixed-format inventory and activation-calibration method as a draft-compression design input. Treat its throughput as a small author benchmark that still needs raw-receipt reproduction, and do not transfer the NVIDIA kernel result to CPU or MI210.

### Dive corrections

- 2026-09-25 Stage 2e: CONFIRMED the submitted GB10 flags and throughput attestation; NARROWED the evidence to a linked author campaign rather than issue-local raw receipts; REATTRIBUTED upstream verification to a source/config check without GB10 reproduction; added independent official-image/source-build and determinism caveats; recorded the later c8 packed-head correctness contradiction as pending direct dives of #35150/#36548. Second-reader correction: added exact official-image and full determinism-section anchors; retained the c8 correctness report as an unresolved material contradiction rather than upstream-verified behavior.

### Verdict

Use the thread as evidence that the fast GB10 recipe is sensitive to image provenance, JIT state, autotuning, memory fraction, and concurrency. Do not accept the throughput cell as production-safe until the packed-head c8 correctness contradiction is resolved.

### Dive corrections

- 2026-09-25 Stage 2e: CONFIRMED pinned SM121 vLLM package identity, tuned MTP summary, README-versus-summary drift, and profile-specific launch/harness contracts; NARROWED the stock-nightly and DFlash2 comparisons because their raw external receipts are not committed in this tree; recorded the upstream DFlash2 PR dependency; confirmed the package supplies no EXL3/CPU/gfx90a execution evidence. Second-reader correction: DFlash2 rates are README-only rather than certification-backed; the 84.28/82.89 c8 conflict is unresolved; harness receipt-copy/parse failures do not fail the suite.

### Verdict

Use this package to separate engine, target-kernel and speculative-decoding contributions and to copy its profile-aware harness. Treat DFlash2 and stock-nightly comparisons as summary-grade until their raw external receipts are incorporated.
